\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\end{pmatrix}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))^{2}\right)_{k+r}^{l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{r}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau^{j}}\frac{\partial\bar{H}^{\prime\prime l}}{\partial\tau^{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial}{\partial w^{k}}\frac{\partial\bar{H}^{\prime\prime l}}{\partial\bar{w}^{k}}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))^{2}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))O(\nabla^{2}_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w})\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))^{2}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))O(\nabla^{2}_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I})).

Combining (
4.1.14
), (
4.1.15
), we get

\|T[0;H^{\prime\prime}]-(\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w})\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\lesssim\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}^{2},\hskip 10.00002pt\forall H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}}.

Since
T:\mathcal{U}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}}\to\mathscr{X}^{-1}
is real-analytic, we see that
\|T[0;H^{\prime\prime}]-T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\|_{\mathscr{X}^{-1}}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{X}^{0}}
. Therefore
\|T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]-(\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w})\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{-1}}\lesssim\|A\|_{\mathscr{X}^{0}}+\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}}
. By taking
\varepsilon_{1}
smaller, we get (
4.1.12
), finishing the proof of
(iii)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.1.6
.

By Lemma
4.1.7

(iii)
,
T:\mathcal{U}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}}\to\mathscr{X}^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q},\mathbb{C}^{m})
is a well-defined real-analytic map for some small
\varepsilon_{1}>0
.

By
(X.3)
, (
4.1.12
) is invertible, whose inverse we denote by
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}:\mathscr{X}^{-1}\to\mathscr{X}^{1}
.

For a given
A\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}}
, we define
\mathcal{S}^{A}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}}\to\mathscr{X}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q},\mathbb{C}^{m})
by
\mathcal{S}^{A}[H^{\prime\prime}]:=H^{\prime\prime}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
. Clearly
\mathcal{S}^{0}[\mathcal{I}]=\mathcal{I}
. By Lemma
4.1.7

(iii)
, since
T
is second order differentiable,
T[0;\mathcal{I}]=0
and
(\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w})\mathcal{I}=0
, we have, for
A\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}}
near
0
and
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}}
near
\mathcal{I}
,

T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]=0+(\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w})(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I})+O_{\mathscr{X}^{1}\to\mathscr{X}^{-1}}((H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I})\otimes(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))+O_{\mathscr{X}^{0}}(A).

Therefore there is a
C_{1}=C_{1}(r,m,q,\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1})>2
that does not depend on
A,H^{\prime\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}^{A}[H^{\prime\prime}]-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}}^{2}+\|A\|_{\mathscr{X}^{0}}),

\displaystyle\forall A\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}},\ H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}};

(4.1.16)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}^{A}[H^{\prime\prime}_{1}]-\mathcal{S}^{A}[H^{\prime\prime}_{2}]\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(\|H_{1}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}}+\|H_{2}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}})\|H_{1}-H_{2}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}},

\displaystyle\forall A\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}},\ H^{\prime\prime}_{1},H^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\varepsilon_{1}}.

Take
\delta_{0}:=\min(\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{1},\frac{1}{6C_{1}^{2}})
. When
A\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathscr{X},\delta_{0}}
, by (
4.1.16
)
\mathcal{S}^{A}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\eta}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\eta}
is a contraction map for all
\frac{4}{3}C_{1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{X}^{0}}<\eta\leq\frac{4}{3}C_{1}{\delta_{0}}
. Therefore there exists a unique fixed point
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\frac{4}{3}C_{1}{\delta_{0}}}
such that
H^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{S}^{A}[H^{\prime\prime}]
. Therefore
H^{\prime\prime}=H^{\prime\prime}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
and thus
T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]=0
. By uniqueness of the fixed point we see that
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathscr{X},\frac{4}{3}C_{1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{X}^{0}}}
, which is
\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}}<\frac{4}{3}C_{1}\|A\|_{\mathscr{X}^{0}}
. Taking
C_{0}:=\frac{4}{3}C_{1}
, we complete the proof.
∎

In application to Theorem
4.1
we have the following:

Corollary 4.1.8
.

Let
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},\infty)\backslash\{1\}
and
\beta>0
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
there is a
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,r,m,q)>0
such that: for any
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
that satisfies
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}}<\delta
, there is a
H^{\prime\prime}
that solves (
4.1.6
) with
H|_{(\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m})\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=\mathcal{I}
. Moreover

(i)

When
\alpha>1
,
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times m})
;

When
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
,
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times m})
.

(ii)

Let
H(\tau,w,s):=(\tau,H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s),s)
. Then
H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
is homeomorphism, and
H(\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{q})\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
.

(iii)

Endow
\mathbb{R}^{r}
and
\mathbb{C}^{m}
with another (real or complex) coordinate system
\sigma=(\sigma^{1},\dots,\sigma^{r})
and
\zeta=(\zeta^{1},\dots,\zeta^{m})
. Let
\tilde{\Phi}:=H^{\mathrm{Inv}}:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
be the inverse map. Then the matrix map
\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
in (
4.1.9
) we have
\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\circ\tilde{\Phi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\sigma,\zeta}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\sigma,\zeta}\mathscr{C}^{\min(\alpha+1,\beta,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta)}_{s}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
with

\|\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\circ\tilde{\Phi}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\min(\alpha+1,\beta,(2-1/\alpha)\beta)}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})}<\varepsilon.

(4.1.17)

Proof.

We set for
k=1,0,-1
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}^{k}

\displaystyle:=\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+k}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{k}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s},

\displaystyle\text{when }\alpha>1

(4.1.18)

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}^{k}

\displaystyle:=\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+k}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{k}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}+\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+k}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{(2-1/\alpha)\beta}_{s},

\displaystyle\text{when }\tfrac{1}{2}<\alpha<1.

Clearly
(X.1)
and
(X.2)
hold. By Lemma
2.2.35
along with a scaling on the
w
-variable we get
(X.3)
. By Corollary
2.2.26

(i)

(ii)
and Proposition
2.2.27
we get
(X.4)
. Thus

\mathscr{X}^{1},\mathscr{X}^{0},\mathscr{X}^{-1}
satisfy the assumptions in Proposition
4.1.6
.

And since
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}\subset\mathscr{X}^{0}
is an embedding. Therefore by Proposition
4.1.6
for a suitable small
\delta_{0}>0
and for
A
such that
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}}<\delta_{0}
, there is a
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{X}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
that solves (
4.1.6
) with
H|_{(\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m})\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=\mathcal{I}
.

From (
4.1.18
) and (
4.1.1
) we see that
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
. By Remark
2.2.10
we have
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}\subset\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\bigcap_{\theta>0}\mathscr{C}^{\theta\alpha}\mathscr{C}^{(1-\theta)\beta}\subset\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}
, thus
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}
when
\alpha>1
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}+\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}\mathscr{C}^{(2-1/\alpha)\beta}\subset\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,(2-1/\alpha)\beta}
when
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
. Thus
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta^{\wedge\alpha}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times m})
and we obtain
(i)
.

By taking
\varepsilon<K_{5}(r+2m,q,\alpha,\beta^{\sim\alpha})^{-1}
where
K_{5}
is the constant in Proposition
2.2.32
, we can find a
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{0})
such that when
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}}<\delta_{1}
we have that
H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
is a bijective
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}
-map and
\|\tilde{\Phi}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{R}^{r+2m+q})}\lesssim 1
. In particular
H
is a homeomorphism. The result
H(\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{q})\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
can be done whenever
\|H-\operatorname{id}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q})}=\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{C^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q})}<\frac{1}{4}
, which is achieved when
\delta_{1}
is suitably small. This proves
(ii)
.

By Lemma
4.1.7

(ii)
we have
\|\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\|_{\mathscr{X}^{0}}\lesssim\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}}+\|A\|_{\mathscr{X}^{0}}\lesssim\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{X}^{1}}+\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}}
. So
\alpha>1
,
(iii)
then follows from Proposition
2.2.36
.

When
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
we have
\mathscr{X}^{0}\subset\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}
, thus by Remark
2.2.10
and Lemma
2.2.31
,

\|\Lambda\circ\tilde{\Phi}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta^{\sim\alpha}}}\lesssim\|\Lambda\circ\tilde{\Phi}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}}\lesssim\|\Lambda\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}}\lesssim\|\Lambda\|_{\mathscr{X}^{0}},

where the implied constant depends on
\|\tilde{\Phi}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{R}^{r+2m+q})}\lesssim 1
. Therefore by keeping track of the constant we get
(iii)
for
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
, finishing the proof.
∎

4.1.3
A scaling argument on parameters

In this part we improve Corollary
4.1.8
to the case where
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}
. In particular we include the discussion of
\alpha=1
.

Proposition 4.1.9
.

Let
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},1]
. Following the Convention
4.1.4
, for any
\varepsilon>0
there is a
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon,\alpha,r,m,q)>0
such that:

For every
\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta\leq\alpha+1
, and for every
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
that satisfies
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q})}<\delta
, there is a
H^{\prime\prime}
that solves (
4.1.6
) with
H|_{(\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m})\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=\mathcal{I}
. Moreover

(i)

H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times m})
.

(ii)

Let
H(\tau,w,s):=(\tau,H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s),s)
. Then
H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
is homeomorphism, and
H(\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{q})\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
.

(iii)

Endow
\mathbb{R}^{r}
and
\mathbb{C}^{m}
with another (real or complex) coordinate system
\sigma=(\sigma^{1},\dots,\sigma^{r})
and
\zeta=(\zeta^{1},\dots,\zeta^{m})
. Let
\tilde{\Phi}:=H^{\mathrm{Inv}}:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
be the inverse map. Then the matrix map
\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
in (
4.1.9
) we have
\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\circ\tilde{\Phi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
with

\|\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\circ\tilde{\Phi}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\varepsilon

(4.1.19)

Remark 4.1.10
.

(i)

The results are also true for
\alpha>1
, where we replace
(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta-
by
\beta-
. We do not state the result for
\alpha>1
because in application to Theorem
4
and
5
we only use the case
\alpha\leq 1
.

(ii)

If we start with a
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}
from the technics in the proof we only obtain
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta-}
. Comparing to Corollary
4.1.8
, where we can get
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta}
, there is a slight regularity loss in the parameter. By doing a more careful Schauder’s estimate in the proof, it is possible to get the estimate
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta}
back.

Proof of Proposition
4.1.9
.

Let
\tilde{C}=\tilde{C}(r,m,\alpha)>0
be maximum of the operator norms of the inclusion maps
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}^{\frac{2\alpha+1}{4}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
and
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{m})\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}^{\frac{2\alpha+1}{4}+1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

We still use the notation
T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
for the left hand side of (
4.1.6
), following from Lemma
4.1.7

(iii)
.

By taking
\mathscr{X}^{j}\in\{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+j}L^{\infty},\mathscr{C}^{\frac{2\alpha+1}{4}+j}L^{\infty}\}
for
j=1,0,-1
in Proposition
4.1.6
, there is a
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(r,m,q,\alpha)>0
and
C_{0}=C_{0}(r,m,q,\alpha)>0
such that

(T.a)

For every
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
such that
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\delta_{0}
there is a unique
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}}<C_{0}\delta_{0}
,
H^{\prime\prime}|_{(\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m})\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=\mathcal{I}
and
T[A;H]=0
. Moreover
\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}}\leq C_{0}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}
.

(T.b)

In particular for every
A_{0}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
such that
\|A_{0}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}<\delta_{0}
there is a unique
H^{\prime\prime}_{0}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that

,

and

.

(T.c)

For every

such that

there is a unique
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\frac{2\alpha+5}{4}}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that

,
H^{\prime\prime}|_{(\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m})\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=\mathcal{I}
and

. Moreover
\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\frac{2\alpha+5}{4}}L^{\infty}}\leq C_{0}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\frac{2\alpha+1}{4}}L^{\infty}}
.

(T.d)

In particular for every
A_{0}\in\mathscr{C}^{\frac{2\alpha+1}{4}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
such that

there is a unique
H^{\prime\prime}_{0}\in\mathscr{C}^{\frac{2\alpha+5}{4}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
\|H^{\prime\prime}_{0}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\frac{2\alpha+1}{4}}}<C_{0}\tilde{C}\delta_{0}
,
H^{\prime\prime}_{0}|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}}=\mathcal{I}
and
T[A_{0};H_{0}]=0
.

Note that by the assumption of
\tilde{C}
,
\|A_{0}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}<\delta_{0}
implies
\|A_{0}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\frac{2\alpha+1}{4}}}<\tilde{C}\delta_{0}

By shrinking

(T.e)

For

(4.1.20)

where

On the other hand by Lemma

(4.1.21)

This gives (

) once

\varepsilon>0
in the assumption.

By direct computation, for each
A
and
H^{\prime\prime}
, the tangent map
\frac{\partial T}{\partial H^{\prime\prime}}[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
is the second differential operator that has the following form: for a function
(F^{1},\dots,F^{m}):\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}\to\mathbb{C}^{m}
and
i=1,\dots,m

\lim_{h\to 0}\frac{T[A;H^{\prime\prime}+hF]^{i}-T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]^{i}}{h}=(\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w})F^{i}+\sum_{j,k=1}^{r+2m}\sum_{u,v=1}^{m}\big(B^{1,iu}_{jk}\partial_{j}(B^{2,uv}_{jk}\partial_{k}F^{v})+B^{3,iu}_{jk}(\partial_{j}B^{4,uv}_{jk})\partial_{k}F^{u}\big).

Here
(\partial_{1},\dots,\partial_{r+2m})=(\partial_{\tau^{1}},\dots,\partial_{\tau^{1}},\partial_{\operatorname{Re}w^{1}},\partial_{\operatorname{Im}w^{1}}\dots,\partial_{\operatorname{Re}w^{m}},\partial_{\operatorname{Im}w^{m}})
. Here
B^{\sigma,uv}_{jk}[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
,
1\leq\sigma\leq 4
,
1\leq u,v\leq m
,
1\leq j,k\leq r+2m
are all rational functions of the components of
A
,
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}
, such that
B^{\sigma,iu}_{jk}(A,\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime},\nabla_{\tau,w}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime})|_{A=0;\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}=\nabla_{\tau,w}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}=\nabla_{\tau,w}\mathcal{I}}=0
.

Taking the first order Taylor expansion of
B^{\sigma,uv}_{jk}(A,\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime},\nabla_{\tau,w}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime})
we see that there is a
C_{2}=C_{2}(r,m,\alpha,\delta_{0})>0
such that for every
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\delta_{0}
and
\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}}<\tilde{C}\delta_{0}
,

\sum_{\sigma=1}^{4}\sum_{u,v=1}^{m}\sum_{j,k=1}^{r+2m}\|B^{\sigma,uv}_{jk}(A,\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime},\nabla_{\tau,w}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq C_{2}(\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}}).

(4.1.22)

By Proposition
2.1.45
with a scaling so that
\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w}
becomes a real Laplacian, there is a
\delta_{1}=\delta_{1}(r,m,\alpha,\delta_{1})\in(0,\delta_{0})

(T.f)

\frac{\partial T}{\partial H^{\prime\prime}}[A_{0};H^{\prime\prime}_{0}]:\{F\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{m}):F|_{\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}}=0\}\to\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{m}),\ \forall\gamma\in(1-\alpha,\alpha].

(4.1.23)

This can be done by taking
\delta_{1}
such that

where

.

Let

, we consider the spaces

\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{j}:=\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-\varepsilon+j}\mathscr{C}^{\frac{\varepsilon\beta}{2\alpha}}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1-\alpha+2\varepsilon+j}\mathscr{C}^{(2-\frac{1+3\varepsilon}{\alpha})\beta},\hskip 10.00002ptj=1,0,-1.

We have

2.2.10

We have the product map
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-\varepsilon}\mathscr{C}^{\frac{\varepsilon\beta}{2\alpha}}\times\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-\varepsilon-1}\mathscr{C}^{\frac{\varepsilon\beta}{2\alpha}}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-\varepsilon-1}\mathscr{C}^{\frac{\varepsilon\beta}{2\alpha}}

. Taking intersections we have
\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{0}\times\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\to\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}
. Therefore
(\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{1},\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{0},\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{-1})
satisfy conditions
(X.1)
-
(X.3)
in Proposition
4.1.6
.

By taking
\gamma\in\{\alpha-\varepsilon,1-\alpha+2\varepsilon\}
in (
4.1.23
) and applying Lemma

, we see that

(T.g)

Let
A_{0}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
and

such that

,
\|H^{\prime\prime}_{0}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}}<C_{0}\delta_{1}
and

. Then the tangent map

\frac{\partial T}{\partial H^{\prime\prime}}[A_{0};H^{\prime\prime}_{0}]:\{F\in\mathscr{X}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}):F|_{(\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m})\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=0\}\to\mathscr{X}^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})\text{ is invertible}.

Therefore by the standard Implicit Function Theorem on Banach Spaces (see for example
[
Edw73
, Page 417]
), or by doing the contraction mapping argument similar to (

), we see that

(T.h)

Let
A_{0}
and
H^{\prime\prime}_{0}
be as in
(T.g)
. There is a
\delta^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,A_{0},H^{\prime\prime}_{0}}=\delta^{\prime}(r,m,q,\alpha,\varepsilon,A_{0},H^{\prime\prime}_{0})>0
and a continuous map
1
1

1

In fact
\mathfrak{h}
does not depend on
\varepsilon,A_{0},H^{\prime\prime}_{0}
but we do not need this property explicitly in the proof.

\hskip-28.90755pt\mathfrak{h}^{A_{0},H_{0}}_{\varepsilon}:\{A\in\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m}):\|A-A_{0}\|_{\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{0}}<\delta^{\prime}\}\to\{H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}):H^{\prime\prime}|_{(\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m})\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=\mathcal{I}\},

for every

Since

in

, which still depends only on

(4.1.24)

We are now ready to prove
(i)
,
(ii)
and

.

Now let
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
be satisfied
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\delta_{1}
, where

is the constant in
(T.g)
. By

, since

, there is a unique

that satisfies the consequences in
(T.a)
.

Since
\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{j}\subset\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+j,\frac{2\alpha-1-2\varepsilon}{2\alpha-1-3\varepsilon}\beta}
and
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+j,\beta-}=\bigcap_{0<\varepsilon<\frac{2\alpha-1}{4}}\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+j,\frac{2\alpha-1-2\varepsilon}{2\alpha-1-3\varepsilon}\beta}
for

, to prove

it suffices to that, for every

and

, there is a

such that

.

Set
A_{0}:=A(\cdot,s_{0})
and
H^{\prime\prime}_{0}:=H^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,s_{0})
. We see that
A_{0}
and
H^{\prime\prime}_{0}
satisfy the assumptions of
(T.g)
.

For
\mu\in(0,1-|s_{0}|)
, we let
A^{s_{0},\mu}(\tau,w,s):=A(\tau,w,\mu(s-s_{0}))
. By Lemma
2.1.12
, we see that

\displaystyle\|A^{s_{0},\mu}-A_{0}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-\varepsilon}\mathscr{C}^{\frac{\varepsilon\beta}{2\alpha}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q})}\lesssim

{}_{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta}\mu^{\min(\frac{1}{2},\frac{\varepsilon\beta}{2\alpha})}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-\varepsilon}\mathscr{C}^{\frac{\varepsilon\beta}{2\alpha}}}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\alpha}\mu^{\min(\frac{1}{2},\frac{\varepsilon\beta}{2\alpha})}\delta_{1};

\displaystyle\|A^{s_{0},\mu}-A_{0}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{1-\alpha+2\varepsilon}\mathscr{C}^{(2-\frac{1+3\varepsilon}{\alpha})\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q})}\lesssim

{}_{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta}\mu^{1-\alpha+2\varepsilon}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{1-\alpha+2\varepsilon}\mathscr{C}^{(2-\frac{1+3\varepsilon}{\alpha})\beta}}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,\alpha}\mu^{1-\alpha+2\varepsilon}\delta_{1}.

In particular there is a small
\mu>0
, such that

\|A^{s_{0},\mu}-A_{0}\|_{\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{0}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q})}<\delta^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,A_{0},H^{\prime\prime}_{0}},

where
\delta^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,A_{0},H^{\prime\prime}_{0}}
is the small constant in
(T.h)
and satisfies (
4.1.24
). Therefore by
(T.h)
, there is a map
\mathfrak{h}^{A_{0},H_{0}}_{\varepsilon}
such that
T[A^{s_{0},\mu};\mathfrak{h}^{A_{0},H_{0}}_{\varepsilon}[A^{s_{0},\mu}]]=0
.

On the other hand we have
T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]=0
. By scaling on
s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}
we see that
H^{\prime\prime s_{0},\mu}(\tau,w,s):=H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,\mu(s-s_{0}))
satisfies
T[A^{s_{0},\mu};H^{\prime\prime}]=0
. Applying the uniqueness of
H^{\prime\prime}
in
(T.c)
and the assumption (
4.1.24
), we see that
\mathfrak{h}^{A_{0},H_{0}}_{\varepsilon}[A^{s_{0},\mu}]\in H^{\prime\prime s_{0},\mu}
.

Therefore
H^{\prime\prime s_{0},\mu}\in\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Taking scaling back we see that
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},B^{q}(s_{0},\mu);\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Since
s_{0}\in\mathbb{B}^{q}
is arbitrary, we get
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{X}_{\varepsilon,\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Since
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, we get
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times m})
. Therefore for every
\delta<\delta_{1}
, we have the result
(i)
.

By
(T.e)
, we see that
H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
is bijective, hence
\tilde{\Phi}
is defined.
By Lemma
2.2.38

(iii)
, since
(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta\leq\alpha+1
, we see that
\tilde{\Phi}
is locally
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta-\varepsilon}
, for every
\varepsilon
near every
(\tau,w,s)\in\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
. Therefore
\tilde{\Phi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,(2-\frac{1}{\alpha})\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q})
, in particular
H
is homeomorphism.

We see that
H(\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{q})\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
whenever
\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\delta+1}L^{\infty}}<\frac{1}{4}
. Thus by shrinking
\delta_{1}
such that
\tilde{C}\delta_{1}<\frac{1}{4}
, we see that when
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\delta_{1}
the result
(ii)
is satisfied.

Finally for every
\varepsilon>0
, by taking
\delta=\min(\delta_{1},\varepsilon/C_{1})
where
C_{1}
is the constant in (
4.1.20
), we get (
4.1.19
) from (
4.1.20
) and we complete the proof.
∎

Remark 4.1.11

(An alternative method via Generalized Hölder-Zygmund spaces)
.

Here the proof of Proposition
4.1.9
is a kind of Schauder’s estimate. Alternatively we can have a different proof use generalized Hölder-Zygmund spaces.

Note that this method does not require the scaling argument Lemma
2.1.12
, and we can also discuss the case
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-,\beta-}
.

Let
(\phi_{j})_{j=0}^{\infty}
be a dyadic resolutions for
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In Lemma
2.1.4
we see that
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
if and only if
\|\phi_{j}\ast f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-j\beta}
. We can generalize the decay/growth rate
2^{-j\beta}
to
\mu(2^{-j})
where
\mu:(0,1]\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
is a certain modulus of smoothness. We define
\mathscr{C}^{\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
to be the space of tempered distributions
f
such that
\|\phi_{j}\ast f\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\mu(2^{-j})
. For example we can pick
\mu(2^{-j})=2^{-j\beta+\sqrt{j}}
, where we have
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}\subsetneq\mathscr{C}^{\mu}\subsetneq\mathscr{C}^{\beta-}
. The
\mathscr{C}^{\mu}
-spaces can naturally be extended to the bi-parameter case.

We assume
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}
and we wish to find a
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta-}
that solves (
4.1.6
).

By assumption we can find modulus of smoothness
\mu
such that
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\mu}
where
\mu:(0,1]\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfies
\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{\log\mu(t)}{\log t}=\beta
, along with some other good properties.

Then we look for spaces
\mathscr{X}^{1},\mathscr{X}^{0},\mathscr{X}^{-1}
that satisfy
(X.1)
-
(X.4)
in Proposition
4.1.6
, such that
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\mu}\subseteq\mathscr{X}^{0}\subseteq\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,(\beta^{\sim\alpha})-}
and
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\mu}\subseteq\mathscr{X}^{1}\subseteq\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,(\beta^{\sim\alpha})-}
. To achieve this, especially to check the condition
(X.4)
, we need to run again the arguments in Section
2.2.2
. For example one needs to prove the paraproduct operators

\mathfrak{P},\mathfrak{R}:\mathscr{C}^{\mu}\times L^{\infty}\to\mathscr{C}^{\mu},

are bounded linear.

Once this is done we know
H\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\mu}\subseteq\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,(\beta^{\sim\alpha})-}
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\mu}\subseteq\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,(\beta^{\sim\alpha})-}
. This completes the sketch of the proof.

For more details of generalized Hölder-Zygmund spaces, we refer
[
Kre16
]
to readers.

4.1.4
The regularity proposition

In this part on a complex space we denote the fixed open cone

\mathbb{H}^{n}=\{x+iy:x,y\in\mathbb{B}^{n},\ 4|y|<1-|x|\}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}_{z}.

(4.1.25)

Clearly
\mathbb{H}^{n}_{z}\cap\mathbb{R}^{n}_{x}=\mathbb{B}^{n}
is a “complex extension” of the unit ball
\mathbb{B}^{n}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
in the real domain.

For a holomorphic function
f\in\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
, we denote
\partial f=\partial_{z}f=(\frac{\partial f}{\partial z^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial f}{\partial z^{n}})
as a
n
-dimensional vector valued function.

In this part we give the proof of the following regularity result for a quasilinear elliptic equation system:

Proposition 4.1.12
.

Let
m,n,p,q\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, let
\Theta:\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{C}^{n\times m}\to\mathbb{C}^{p}
be a complex bilinear map, and let
L=\sum_{j=1}^{n}a^{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}
be a vector-valued first order differential operator on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with constant matrix coefficients
a^{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{m\times p}
.

Assume
\alpha>\frac{1}{2}
, there is a
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(m,n,p,q,\alpha,\Theta,L)>0
that satisfies the following:

Let
\tilde{\beta}\in\{\gamma,\gamma-:\gamma\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\}
. Suppose
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{x}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{x}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}_{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
satisfies
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{x}L^{\infty}_{s}}<\varepsilon_{0}
and
\Delta_{x}f=L_{x}\Theta(f,\partial_{x}f)
, then
f
admits extension
\mathfrak{f}:\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{m}
such that
\mathfrak{f}
is holomorphic in
z
and
\mathfrak{f}(x+i0;s)=f(x;s)
for
x\in\mathbb{B}^{n},s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}
.
Moreover
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

In application we take
\tilde{\beta}=\beta^{\sim\alpha}=\min(\beta,(2-1/\alpha)\beta,\alpha+1)
. We note that
\varepsilon_{0}
can be choose independent of
\tilde{\beta}
.

The proof of Proposition
4.1.12
is based on Proposition
2.4.1
. Here we need the parameter version of the spaces
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
, which are the natural generalizations to Definition
2.4.3
:

Definition 4.1.13
.

Let
\alpha<1
.
We define
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})=\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})
as the set of
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})
such that
f(\cdot,s)\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
for almost every
s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}
and
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}:=\mathop{\operatorname{essup}}_{s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}}\|f(\cdot,s)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}}<\infty
.

For
\beta>0
we define
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q}):=\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n}))
.

We define the space
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-\eta}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})
with norm
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-\eta}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}:=\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}+\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-\eta}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}
, for
\eta<1
.

Remark 4.1.14
.

By Lemmas
2.1.3
and
2.2.3

(ii)
, we see that
\mathbf{P},\tilde{\mathbf{P}},\mathbf{E}
in Proposition
2.4.1
have natural extension to the parameter case. We still denote them as
\mathbf{P},\tilde{\mathbf{P}},\mathbf{E}
: let
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{q}
be a bounded smooth subset, we have boundedness:

•

\mathbf{P}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{n},V)\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{n},V)
for
-2<\alpha<1
and
\mathscr{X}\in\{L^{\infty},\mathscr{C}^{\beta}:\beta>0\}
.

•

\tilde{\mathbf{P}}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},V)\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},V)
for
-2<\alpha<1
and
\mathscr{X}\in\{L^{\infty},\mathscr{C}^{\beta}:\beta>0\}
.

•

\mathbf{E}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{n},V)\cap\ker\Delta_{x}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},V)
for
-2<\alpha<1
and
\mathscr{X}\in\{L^{\infty},\mathscr{C}^{\beta}:\beta>0\}
.

Moreover, their operator norms equal to
\|\mathbf{P}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}
,
\|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})}
and
\|\mathbf{E}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\cap\ker\Delta\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{H}^{n})}
, respectively.

The following lemma is useful in the proof of Proposition
4.1.12
.

Lemma 4.1.15
.

Let
\beta>0
and let
\mathscr{X}\in\{L^{\infty},\mathscr{C}^{\beta}\}
.

(i)

The differentiation
[\tilde{f}\mapsto\partial_{z}\tilde{f}]:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{n})
is bounded linear for all
\alpha<1
.

(ii)

For
\alpha<0
,
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})
has an equivalent norm

\textstyle\tilde{f}\mapsto\sup_{z\in\mathbb{H}^{n}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{-\alpha}\|\tilde{f}(z,\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})}.

(4.1.26)

(iii)

The restriction map
[\tilde{f}\mapsto\tilde{f}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}]:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{x}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})
is bounded linear for
0<\alpha<1
.

(iv)

We have inclusion map
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})
for
0<\alpha<1
.

Moreover all operator norms above are not depend on
\beta
.

Proof.

These results follow from Lemma
2.4.4
, the parameter free cases, along with Lemma
2.1.3
when
\mathscr{X}=\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
, and the definition when
\mathscr{X}=L^{\infty}
.
∎

We need to show that in
\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
,
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\Theta(\mathfrak{f},\partial_{z}\mathfrak{f})
maps
\mathfrak{f}
into the desired function space.

Lemma 4.1.16
.

Let
0<\alpha<1
and
\beta>0
.
For
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})
and
g\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})
, we have
fg\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})
. Moreover there is a
C=C(n,q,\alpha)>0
that does not depend on
\beta
and
f,g
such that

\|fg\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}\leq C\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}.

(4.1.27)

Proof.

Clearly the product
f(z,s)g(z,s)
is locally continuous and holomorphic in
z
.

By Lemma
4.1.15

(ii)
and
(iv)
, we have almost every
s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}
,

\displaystyle|(fg)(z,s)|\lesssim_{n,\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{\alpha-1}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{n,\alpha}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{\alpha-1}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}.

Dividing
\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{\alpha-1}
on both side, taking essential sup over
z
and
s
, and using Lemma
4.1.15

(ii)
we get the first control
\|fg\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
.

To control
\|fg\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
. By Lemmas
2.1.17

(ii)
and
4.1.15

(ii)
, for every
z\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\|fg(z,\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{q})}\leq\|f(z,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g(z,\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}+\|f(z,\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|g(z,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{-2}+\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{-1}\cdot\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{\alpha-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{-2}.

Dividing
\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{-2}
on both side, we get
\|fg\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
, completing the proof.
∎

Remark 4.1.17
.

By considering higher derivatives we can define
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}
for
\alpha\geq 1
naturally, where Lemma
4.1.16
still holds. On the other hand by Example
2.2.29
, we see that
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}
in (
4.1.27
) cannot be replaced by
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
unless
\alpha>2
. This is partly because the following: for
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}
-functions the high frequency information only occur near the boundary, whereas for
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-functions the singularity are allowed to be “evenly spread”.

Proof of Proposition
4.1.12
.

It suffices to consider
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
and
\tilde{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

First we claim that by possibly shrinking
\varepsilon_{0}>0
we can assume that
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}<\varepsilon_{0}
.

Let
s_{0}\in\mathbb{B}^{q}
be a fixed point, to prove the proposition it suffices to show that there exists a
\delta>0
such that
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{B}^{n},B^{q}(s_{0},\delta);\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

For
0<\mu\leq 1-|s_{0}|
, let
f_{s_{0},\mu}(x,s):=f(x,\mu(s-s_{0}))
be a function defined on
(x,s)\in\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
. By Lemma
2.1.12
we see that

\lim\limits_{\mu\to 0}\|f_{s_{0},\mu}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\leq\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}+\|f(\cdot,s_{0})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{C}^{m})}.

Since
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}\subset C^{0}
, we have
\|f(\cdot,s_{0})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|f(\cdot,s_{0})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})}\leq\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}
. Therefore, if
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\varepsilon_{0}
, then
\lim\limits_{\mu\to 0}\|f_{s_{0},\mu}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}<C_{\alpha}\varepsilon_{0}
where
C_{\alpha}
does not depend on
\varepsilon_{0}
.

Thus we can find a
\mu\in(0,1-|s_{0}|]
such that
\|f_{s_{0},\mu}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}<2C_{\alpha}\varepsilon_{0}
. By replacing
\varepsilon_{0}
with
2C_{\alpha}\varepsilon_{0}
, we have a
\mathfrak{f}_{s_{0},\mu}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
\mathfrak{f}_{\mu,s_{0}}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=f_{\mu,s_{0}}
. Taking scaling back we get
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},B^{q}(s_{0},\mu);\mathbb{C}^{m})
, completing the proof of the claim.

Now we assume
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}<\varepsilon_{0}
, where
\varepsilon_{0}>0
is a small number to be determined.

We use
\Theta[u]=\Theta(u,\nabla_{x}u)
for function
u
either on
\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
or on
\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
. Note that for
\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}
we have
\nabla_{x}\tilde{u}=\partial_{z}\tilde{u}
.

The proof uses contraction mappings twice. The first one take place in
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and shows that
\mathbf{P}\Delta f
has some particular properties. The second one take place in
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, showing that
\mathbf{P}\Delta f
is a restriction of a function in
\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
which is holomorphic in
z
.

For
u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, by Lemma
2.1.17
(since
\alpha>\frac{1}{2}
) we have
u\otimes\nabla_{x}u(\cdot,s)\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{x}
uniformly for almost every
s
, so
\Theta[u]\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{p})
and thus
L\Theta[u]\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Moreover we have for every
u,v\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})

\displaystyle\|L\Theta[u]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Theta(u,\nabla_{x}u)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|u\otimes\nabla_{x}u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}L^{\infty}}^{2},

(4.1.28)

\displaystyle\|L\Theta[u]-L\Theta[v]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Theta(u-v,\nabla_{x}u)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}}+\|\Theta(v,\nabla_{x}(u-v))\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}}\lesssim(\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+\|v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}})\|u-v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}.

Here the implicit constants depend only on
m,n,q,\alpha,L,\Theta
but not on
u
or
v
.

For
\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,
L\Theta[\tilde{u}]
is a function on
\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
that is holormorphic in
z
. By Lemma
4.1.16
,
\tilde{u}\otimes\partial_{z}\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}n})
, we know
L\Theta[\tilde{u}]\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-3}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Moreover, similar to (
4.1.29
) we have for
\tilde{u},\tilde{v}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
:

\displaystyle\|L\Theta[\tilde{u}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-3}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}^{2},

(4.1.29)

\displaystyle\|L\Theta[\tilde{u}]-L\Theta[\tilde{v}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-3}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha}(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}})\|\tilde{u}-\tilde{v}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}.

Here the implicit constants depend only on
m,n,q,\alpha,L,\Theta
but not on
\tilde{\beta}
,
\tilde{u}
or
\tilde{v}
.

Let
\mathbf{P}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
be as in Proposition
2.4.1
. So the assumption
\Delta f=L\Theta[f]
implies that

f=f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f+\mathbf{P}\Delta f=(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)+\mathbf{P}L\Theta[\mathbf{P}\Delta f+(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)].

(4.1.30)

Define
T_{f}
on
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
as

T_{f}[u]:=(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)+\mathbf{P}L\Theta[u+(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)],\hskip 10.00002ptu\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}).

Since
\|f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}
. By (
4.1.28
) and Remark
4.1.14
, we can find a
C_{1}=C_{1}(m,n,q,\alpha,L,\Theta,\mathbf{P})>1
that does not depend on
f
, that bounds (
4.1.28
) and satisfies
C_{1}>\|\operatorname{id}-\mathbf{P}\Delta\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}
. More precisely, for every
f,u,v\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q},\mathbb{C}^{m})
, we have

\displaystyle\|T_{f}[u]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+C_{1}\big(\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\big)^{2};

\displaystyle\|T_{f}[u]-T_{f}[v]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left(\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+\|v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\right)\|u-v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}};

\displaystyle\|f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}.

Take
\varepsilon_{1}>0
such that
9\varepsilon_{1}C_{1}+10\varepsilon_{1}C_{1}^{2}<1
. If
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\varepsilon_{1}
and
\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}},\|v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq 2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\|T_{f}[u]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}+C_{1}(3C_{1}\varepsilon_{1})^{2}<2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1},

\displaystyle\|T_{f}[u]-T_{f}[v]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<C_{1}(5C_{1}\varepsilon_{1})\|u-v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\tfrac{1}{2}\|u-v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}.

Therefore when
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\varepsilon_{1}
,
T_{f}
is a contraction mapping in
\{u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}):\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq 2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}\}
. In such case, there is a unique
u
satisfying
\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq 2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}
such that
T_{f}[u]=u
. On the other hand,
f\in\{u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}):\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq 2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}\}
satisfies (
4.1.30
). Thus
f
is that unique fixed point.

Similarly, by Proposition
2.4.1

(i)
(also see Remark
4.1.14
), we have boundedness
\mathbf{P}:\mathscr{C}^{-3}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
, so
\|f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}
. Note that
f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f
is harmonic function in
x
-variable, so by Proposition
2.4.1

(iii)
and Remark
4.1.14
we have
\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
with

\|\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}.

(4.1.31)

For a
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, define
\tilde{T}_{f}
on
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
as

\tilde{T}_{f}[\tilde{u}]=\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)+\tilde{\mathbf{P}}L\Theta[\tilde{u}+\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)],\hskip 10.00002pt\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}).

Here
\tilde{\mathbf{P}}
is as in Proposition
2.4.1

(ii)
.

Take
C_{2}=C_{2}(m,n,q,\alpha,L,\Theta,\tilde{\mathbf{P}},\mathbf{E})>1
that bounds (
4.1.29
) and (
4.1.31
). Therefore, for every
\tilde{u},\tilde{v}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{T}_{f}[\tilde{u}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}+C_{2}\big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}+C_{2}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}\big)^{2};

\displaystyle\|\tilde{T}_{f}[\tilde{u}]-\tilde{T}_{f}[\tilde{v}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}+C_{2}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}\big)\|\tilde{u}-\tilde{v}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}.

By Lemma
4.1.15

(iii)
we can find a
C_{3}=C_{3}(m,n,q,\alpha)>0
such that,

\|\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\leq C_{3}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})},\hskip 10.00002pt\forall u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}).

(4.1.32)

Take
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that
C_{3}C_{2}\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon_{1}
and
9\varepsilon_{0}C_{2}+10\varepsilon_{0}C_{2}^{2}<1
. So
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}<\varepsilon_{0}
implies
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\varepsilon_{1}
.
For such
f
, we know
\tilde{T}_{f}
is a contraction mapping in
\{\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}):\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}}\leq 2C_{2}\varepsilon_{0}\}
.
Thus there is a unique
\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}}\leq 2C_{2}\varepsilon_{0}
and
\tilde{u}=\tilde{T}_{f}[\tilde{u}]
.

By Proposition
2.4.1

(ii)
,

\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=\big(\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)+\tilde{\mathbf{P}}L\Theta[\tilde{u}+\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)]\big)\big|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f+\mathbf{P}L\Theta[\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}+f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f]=T_{f}[\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}].

By (
4.1.32
) and the assumption
C_{3}C_{2}\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon_{1}<2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}
, we have
\|\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\leq 2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}
. By the uniqueness of the fixed point for
T_{f}
, we get
f=\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}
.

So
\mathfrak{f}=\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
is the desired extension of
f
to
\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
.

Finally, by Lemma
2.2.22
we have
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})\subset\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, completing the proof.
∎

4.1.5
Some transition lemmas

We first construct the coordinate chart
L
.

Lemma 4.1.18
.

Endow
\mathbb{R}^{r},\mathbb{C}^{m},\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard coordinate systems
\tau=(\tau^{1},\dots,\tau^{r})
,
w=(w^{1},\dots,w^{m})
,
s=(s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
respectively.

Let
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},\infty)
,
\beta\in(0,\infty)
. Let
\mathcal{S}\leq(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}
and
(u_{0},v_{0})\in\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N}
be as in the assumption of Theorem
4.1
. Then for any
\delta>0
there are neighborhoods
U_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
of
p_{0}
,
V_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{N}
of
q_{0}
and smooth coordinate charts
(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime}):U_{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w}
,
L^{\prime\prime\prime}:V_{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
, such that for the product chart
L=(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime},L^{\prime\prime\prime})
,
L_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a local basis
X=[X_{1},\dots,X_{r+m}]^{\top}
with the form (
4.1.3
) defined on
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
, and

\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})}<\delta.

(4.1.33)

Proof.

Let
\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}:\tilde{V}_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{N}\to\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
be an arbitrary smooth coordinate chart near
v_{0}
such that
\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}(v_{0})=0
. Since
\operatorname{rank}\mathcal{S}=r+m
and
\operatorname{rank}(\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}})=r+2m
, by standard linear algebra argument (see also
[
BCH08
, Page 18]
) we can find a smooth coordinate chart
(\tilde{L}^{\prime},\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime}):\tilde{U}_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w}
such that

\textstyle(\tilde{L}^{\prime},\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime})(u_{0})=(0,0)\text{ and }\mathcal{S}_{(u_{0},v_{0})}=(\tilde{L}^{\prime},\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime})^{*}\operatorname{Span}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau^{r}},\frac{\partial}{\partial w^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial w^{m}}\big)\big|_{u_{0}}\leq\mathbb{C}T_{u_{0}}\mathfrak{M}.

(4.1.34)

Therefore, by considering
\tilde{L}:=(\tilde{L}^{\prime},\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime},\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime\prime})
and working on
\mathcal{S}_{(u_{0},v_{0})}\leq\mathbb{C}T_{(u_{0},v_{0})}(\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N})
, we have

\textstyle(\tilde{L}_{*}\mathcal{S})_{(0,0,0)}\oplus\operatorname{Span}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}^{1}}\big|_{(0,0,0)},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}^{m}}\big|_{(0,0,0)},\frac{\partial}{\partial s^{1}}\big|_{(0,0,0)},\dots\frac{\partial}{\partial s^{q}}\big|_{(0,0,0)}\big)=\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}.

(4.1.35)

Applying Lemma
2.3.9
, since we have (
4.1.35
), there is a
\lambda_{0}>0
such that
\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\subseteq\tilde{L}(U_{0}\times V_{0})
and we can find a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-local basis
\tilde{X}=[\tilde{X}_{1},\dots,\tilde{X}_{r+m}]^{\top}
of
\tilde{L}_{*}\mathcal{S}
that is defined on
\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
and has the form,

\tilde{X}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{X}^{\prime}\\
\tilde{X}^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}&&\tilde{A}^{\prime}&\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime\prime}\\
&I_{m}&\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime}&\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tau}\\
\partial_{w}\\
\partial_{\bar{w}}\\
\partial_{s}\end{pmatrix},\hskip 10.00002pt\text{on }\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}.

Clearly
\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime\prime}=0
and
\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}=0
since
\mathcal{S}\leq(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}
. By (
4.1.34
) we have
\tilde{A}^{\prime}(0)=0
and
\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime}(0)=0
.

Write
\tilde{A}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{A}^{\prime}\\
\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}
. By Lemma
2.1.12
there is a
C=C(r,m,q,\lambda_{0},\alpha,\beta)>0
such that

\|\tilde{A}(\lambda\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})}\leq C\lambda^{\min(\beta,\frac{1}{2})}\|\tilde{A}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})},\hskip 10.00002pt\forall\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0}].

(4.1.36)

Take
\lambda=(C\|\tilde{A}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q})})^{-2}\cdot(\frac{\delta}{2})^{2}
. Then the right hand side of (
4.1.36
) is less than
\delta
.

Take
L:=\lambda\tilde{L}
, we see that
L_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}
-local basis
X=[X_{1},\dots,X_{r+m}]^{\top}
with the form

X=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}&&A^{\prime}\\
&I_{m}&A^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tau}\\
\partial_{w}\\
\partial_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix},\hskip 10.00002pt\text{where }\begin{pmatrix}A^{\prime}(\tau,w,s)\\
A^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{A}^{\prime}(\lambda\tau,\lambda w,\lambda s)\\
\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda\tau,\lambda w,\lambda s)\end{pmatrix}\text{ for }(\tau,w)\in\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}.

So we have
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q})}=\|\tilde{A}(\lambda\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q})}<\delta
as desired.
∎

Next we show that (
4.1.5
) and (
4.1.6
) are associated. Here we endow
\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}
with two standard coordinate systems
(\tau,w)=(\tau^{1},\dots,\tau^{r},w^{1},\dots,w^{m})
and
(\sigma,\zeta)=(\sigma^{1},\dots,\sigma^{r},\zeta^{1},\dots,\zeta^{m})
, and we still use
s=(s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
as the standard coordinate system for
\mathbb{R}^{q}
.

Lemma 4.1.19
.

Let
\varepsilon,\delta>0
,
A:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m}
and
H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
be as in (the assumptions and consequence) of Proposition
4.1.6
. Then,

(i)

If
L_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a local basis with the form (
4.1.3
), then
(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a local basis with the form (
4.1.4
).

(ii)

Suppose
\mathcal{S}
is involutive and
H^{\prime\prime}
is a solution to (
4.1.6
), then the coefficient matrix
B=[B^{\prime},B^{\prime\prime}]^{\top}
in (
4.1.4
) is a solution to (
4.1.7
).

Proof.

Following Convention
4.1.4
, by assumption
H(\tau,w,s)=(\tau,H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s),s)
, we have

H^{*}(d\sigma,d\zeta,d\bar{\zeta},ds)=(d\tau,dw,d\bar{w},ds)\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{s}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{s}&I\end{pmatrix}\ \Rightarrow\ \begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}H^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\\
H^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta}\\
H^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\end{pmatrix}.

(4.1.37)

Therefore taking pushforward of (
4.1.3
) under
H
we have,

H_{*}X=H_{*}\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&&A^{\prime}\\
&I&A^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\end{pmatrix}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\circ H^{\mathrm{Inv}}\right)\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\end{pmatrix}.

The matrix
\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}
is invertible (see Lemma
4.1.7

(i)
), we see that
H_{*}(L_{*}\mathcal{S})=(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a local basis

H_{*}\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}X^{\prime}\\
X^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\right)=\left(\left(I_{r+m}+\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)\circ H^{\mathrm{Inv}}\right)\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\end{pmatrix}.

This gives (
4.1.4
) and finishes the proof of
(i)
.

Recall the notations
B^{\prime}=(b_{j}^{l})_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq r;1\leq l\leq m\end{subarray}}
and
B^{\prime\prime}=(b_{r+k}^{l})_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k,l\leq m\end{subarray}}
. By (
4.1.4
) we have,

b_{j}^{l}\circ H=\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{j}^{l},\hskip 10.00002pt1\leq j\leq m+r,\hskip 10.00002pt1\leq l\leq m.

(4.1.38)

By (
4.1.37
) we have, for
1\leq j\leq r
and
1\leq k\leq m
,

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma^{j}}\right|_{H(\tau,w,s)}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\;\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{j},\ \left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{k}}\right|_{H(\tau,w,s)}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\;\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{r+m+k}.

(4.1.39)

Plugging (
4.1.38
) and (
4.1.39
) into (
4.1.6
) we get
\sum_{j=1}^{r}\frac{\partial b_{j}^{l}}{\partial\sigma^{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial b_{r+k}^{l}}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{k}}=0
for
1\leq l\leq m
, which is the last row of (
4.1.7
).

Since
\mathcal{S}
is involutive,
(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}
is also involutive, by Lemma
2.3.9

(iii)
,
T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
given in (
4.1.4
) are pairwise commutative. Writing out the condition
[T_{j},T_{j^{\prime}}]=0

(1\leq j<j^{\prime}\leq r)
,
[T_{j},Z_{k}]=0

(1\leq j\leq r,1\leq k\leq m)
and
[Z_{k},Z_{k^{\prime}}]=0

(1\leq k<k^{\prime}\leq m)
in the coordinate components we get the first three rows of (
4.1.7
), finishing the proof of
(ii)
.
∎

Now we have the PDE system (
4.1.7
). We show that it satisfies the assumption of Proposition
4.1.12
.

Lemma 4.1.20
.

Suppose
B:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m}
solves (
4.1.7
), then
f(\sigma,\xi,\eta,s):=B(\sigma,\frac{1}{2}(\xi+i\eta),s)
is defined on
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
and
f
solves a PDE with the form
Lf=\Delta_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}\Theta(f,\nabla_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}f)
where
L
and
\Theta
satisfy the assumptions of Proposition
4.1.12
.

Proof.

We can write (
4.1.7
) as the form
DB=\tilde{\Theta}(B,\nabla_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}B)
where the coefficients of
D
and
\Theta
take value in
\mathbb{C}^{m\times\frac{(r+m)(r+m-1)}{2}+m}
.

By direct computation (see also
[
Str20b
, Lemma B.5]
) we have
D^{*}D=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{j}\partial\sigma^{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\zeta^{k}\partial\bar{\zeta}^{k}}=\Delta_{\sigma}+\Box_{\zeta}
, acting on the components of the vector value functions.

Using the real coordinates
\xi=\operatorname{Re}\zeta
,
\eta=\operatorname{Im}\zeta
, we see that
\Box_{\zeta}=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\xi^{k}\partial\xi^{k}}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\eta^{k}\partial\eta^{k}})=\frac{1}{4}\Delta_{\xi,\eta}
. So for
f(\sigma,\xi,\eta,s)=B(\sigma,\frac{\xi+i\eta}{2},s)
we have
(D^{*}DB)(\sigma,\frac{\xi+i\eta}{2},s)=(\Delta_{\sigma}+\Delta_{\xi,\eta})f(\sigma,\xi,\eta,s)
and
\nabla_{\xi,\eta}f(\sigma,\xi,\eta,s)=\frac{1}{2}(\nabla_{\xi,\eta}B)(\sigma,\frac{\xi+i\eta}{2},s)
. Therefore
f
satisfies
\Delta_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}f=D^{*}\tilde{\Theta}(f,(\nabla_{\sigma}f,2\nabla_{\xi,\eta}f))
. Taking
L:=D^{*}
and
\Theta(f,\nabla_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}f):=\tilde{\Theta}(f,(\nabla_{\sigma}f,2\nabla_{\xi,\eta}f))
we finish the proof.
∎

Finally we find the map
G^{\prime\prime}
in (
4.1.2
) using Proposition
2.3.45
. We work on
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}
and its “complex extension”
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{H}^{r+2m}\subset\mathbb{C}^{r+2m}
.

We endow
\mathbb{C}^{r+2m}
with standard complex coordinates
(\boldsymbol{\sigma},\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\eta}^{m})
in the way that
\sigma=\operatorname{Re}\boldsymbol{\sigma}
,
\xi=\operatorname{Re}\boldsymbol{\xi}
and
\eta=\operatorname{Re}\boldsymbol{\eta}
. We denote
\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\zeta}}:=\frac{1}{2}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\xi}}-i\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\eta}})=[\frac{1}{2}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1}}-i\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1}}),\dots,\frac{1}{2}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m}}-i\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\eta}^{m}})]^{\top}
.

Note that in this place, we temporarily forget the original complex structure of
\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\zeta}
. The objects
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=\boldsymbol{\xi}+i\boldsymbol{\eta}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\zeta}}
are just conventions.

In this setting,
T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
given in (
4.1.4
) are complex vector fields defined on
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
.

Lemma 4.1.21
.

Let
\tilde{\beta}\in\{\gamma,\gamma-:\gamma\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\}
. Suppose
T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
in (
4.1.4
) are commutative, and the coefficient matrix function
B:\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m}
has a
(\sigma,\xi,\eta)
-variable holomorphic extension
\mathfrak{B}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{H}^{r+2m},\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
. Then for any
(\sigma_{0},\xi_{0},\eta_{0},s_{0})\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
there is a neighborhood
U_{1}\times V_{1}\subset\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
of
(\sigma_{0},\xi_{0},\eta_{0},s_{0})
and a map
G^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(U_{1},V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
G^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{0},\xi_{0},\eta_{0},s_{0})=0
and

(\operatorname{Span}(T_{1}(\cdot,s),\dots,T_{r}(\cdot,s),Z_{1}(\cdot,s),\dots,Z_{m}(\cdot,s)))^{\bot}|_{U_{1}}=\operatorname{Span}(dG^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,s)^{1},\dots,dG^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,s)^{m})\leq\mathbb{C}T^{*}U_{1},\hskip 10.00002pt\forall\ s\in V_{1}.

Recall the notation of dual bundle in (
2.3.6
).

Proof.

We define collections of vector fields
\mathfrak{T}=[\mathfrak{T}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{T}_{r}]^{\top}
and
\mathfrak{Z}=[\mathfrak{Z}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{Z}_{m}]^{\top}
as

\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{T}\\
\mathfrak{Z}\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}&&\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\\
&I_{m}&\mathfrak{B}^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\\
\partial_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\\
\partial_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}}\end{pmatrix}.

Here
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
is the upper
r\times m
block of
\mathfrak{B}
, and
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime\prime}
is the lower
m\times m
block of
\mathfrak{B}
.

Thus
(T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m})
is the “real domain restriction” of
(\mathfrak{T}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{T}_{r},\mathfrak{Z}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{Z}_{m})
in the sense of Proposition
2.3.45
. We see that the coefficients of
[\mathfrak{T}_{j},\mathfrak{T}_{j^{\prime}}]
,
[\mathfrak{T}_{j},\mathfrak{Z}_{k}]
and
[\mathfrak{Z}_{k},\mathfrak{Z}_{k^{\prime}}]
are the holomorphic extension those of
[T_{j},T_{j^{\prime}}]
,
[T_{j},Z_{k}]
and
[Z_{k},Z_{k^{\prime}}]
.

By assumption
T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
are commutative, so
[T_{j},T_{j^{\prime}}]=[T_{j},Z_{k}]=[Z_{k},Z_{k^{\prime}}]=0
. Since a holomorphic function in
\mathbb{H}^{r+2m}
that vanishes in
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}
must be a zero function, we know
[\mathfrak{T}_{j},\mathfrak{T}_{j^{\prime}}]=[\mathfrak{T}_{j},\mathfrak{Z}_{k}]=[\mathfrak{Z}_{k},\mathfrak{Z}_{k^{\prime}}]=0
as well. Thus
\mathfrak{T}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{T}_{r},\mathfrak{Z}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{Z}_{m}
are commutative.

Now the assumptions of Proposition
2.3.45
are satisfied. By Proposition
2.3.45
we can find a neighborhood
\tilde{U}_{1}\times V_{1}\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{H}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
of
(\sigma_{0},\xi_{0},\eta_{0},s_{0})
and a map
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(\tilde{U}_{1},V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{0},\xi_{0},\eta_{0},s_{0})=0
and for
U_{1}:=\tilde{U}_{1}\cap\mathbb{R}^{r+2m}
we have for each
s\in V_{1}
,

(\operatorname{Span}(T_{1}(\cdot,s),\dots,T_{r}(\cdot,s),Z_{1}(\cdot,s),\dots,Z_{m}(\cdot,s)))^{\bot}|_{U_{1}}=\operatorname{Span}(d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,s)^{1}|_{U_{1}}),\dots,d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,s)^{m}|_{U_{1}}))\leq\mathbb{C}T^{*}U_{1}.

Clearly
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}|_{U_{1}\times V_{1}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}(U_{1},V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{m})
holds. Thus,
G^{\prime\prime}:=\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}|_{U_{1}\times V_{1}}
is as desired.
∎

4.1.6
Proof of Theorem
4.1

In the proof we often consider the local bases of cotangent subbundles.

Convention 4.1.22
.

Let
M
and
N
be two smooth manifolds, let
\mathcal{T}\leq(\mathbb{C}TM)\times N
be a continuous subbundle. Let
\mu=(\mu^{1},\dots,\mu^{n}):M\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\nu=(\nu^{1},\dots,\nu^{q}):N\to\mathbb{R}^{q}
be two smooth coordinate charts and let
f=(f^{1},\dots,f^{n-r}):M\times N\to\mathbb{C}^{n-r}
be a continuous map which is
C^{1}
in
M
. We say
\mathcal{T}^{\bot}
is spanned by
df^{1},\dots,df^{n-r},d\nu^{1},\dots,d\nu^{q}
, denoted by
\mathcal{T}^{\bot}=\operatorname{Span}(df,d\nu)
, if

\operatorname{Span}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial f^{i}}{\partial\mu^{j}}(x,s)d\mu^{j}|_{x}\Big)_{i=1}^{r}=\{\theta\in T^{*}_{x}M:\langle\theta,X\rangle=0,\ \forall X\in\mathcal{T}_{(x,s)}\},\hskip 10.00002pt\forall\ u\in M,\ s\in N.

(4.1.40)

Remark 4.1.23
.

When
\tilde{\beta}>1
and when
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\tilde{\beta}}
, (
4.1.40
) coincides with the classical notion
\mathcal{T}^{\bot}=\operatorname{Span}(df,d\nu)
since
\operatorname{Span}(df,d\nu)=\operatorname{Span}(\frac{\partial f}{\partial\mu}d\mu+\frac{\partial f}{\partial\nu}d\nu,d\nu)=\operatorname{Span}(\frac{\partial f}{\partial\mu}d\mu,d\nu)
.

The convention is used specifically for the case
\tilde{\beta}\leq 1
, since when
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\tilde{\beta}}
, the collection of differentials
df
is no longer pointwise defined, while
\frac{\partial f}{\partial\mu}\cdot d\mu\equiv df\pmod{d\nu}
and the left hand side is still pointwise.

We now begin the proof.
On the space
\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\sigma}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\zeta}
, we write
\xi=\operatorname{Re}\zeta
and
\eta=\operatorname{Im}\zeta
. Recall the notation
\beta^{\sim\alpha}
and
\beta^{\wedge\alpha}
in (
4.1.1
).

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.
By Lemma
4.1.20
and Proposition
4.1.12
, along with a scaling, we can find a
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(r,m,q,\alpha)>0
such that,

(B.1)

If
B\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta^{\sim\alpha}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\eta,\xi},\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
satisfies (
4.1.7
) with

\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q})}<\varepsilon_{0},

(4.1.41)

then
B(\sigma,\frac{\xi+i\eta}{2},s)
has a
(\sigma,\xi,\eta)
-holomorphic extension
\mathfrak{B}(\boldsymbol{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\xi},\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\eta},s)\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta^{\sim\alpha}}(\mathbb{H}^{r+2m}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}},\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
.

For such
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, we can find a
\delta_{0}>0
such that the results of Proposition
4.1.6
are satisfied with
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}
,
\delta=\delta_{0}
.

For such
\delta_{0}>0
, by Lemma
4.1.18
there is a smooth coordinate chart
L:=((L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime}),L^{\prime\prime\prime}):U_{0}\times V_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
near
(u_{0},v_{0})
such that,

(L.1)

(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime}):U_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}
and
L^{\prime\prime\prime}:V_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{N}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
are both smooth coordinate charts, and
L(u_{0},v_{0})=(0,0)
.

(L.2)

L_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}
-local basis with form (
4.1.3
), such that:

•

When
\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, (
4.1.33
) is satisfied with
\delta=\delta_{0}
, i.e.
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})}<\delta_{0}
.

•

When
\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{-}(=\{\gamma-:\gamma\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\})
,
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})}<\delta_{0}
.

By Corollary
4.1.8
if
\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
\alpha\neq 1
, and Proposition
4.1.9
if
\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{-}
or
\alpha=1
, we can find a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}
-map
H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\sigma}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
that has the form
H(\tau,w,s)=(\tau,H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s),s)
such that

(H.1)

H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
is homeomorphism, and
H,H^{\mathrm{Inv}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

(H.2)

H(\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{q})\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
.

(H.3)

H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta^{\wedge\alpha}}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times m})
.

(H.4)

H^{\prime\prime}
solves (
4.1.6
).

(H.5)

When
\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
\alpha\neq 1
, (
4.1.17
) is satisfied with
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}
.

When
\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{-}
or
\alpha=1
, (
4.1.19
) is satisfied with
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}
.

Here
(H.1)
shows that
(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}
is defined on
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
.

By Lemma
4.1.19

(ii)
,
(H.4)
implies that
B
solves (
4.1.7
). By comparing (
4.1.9
) and (
4.1.4
) we see that
B=\Lambda[A,H^{\prime\prime}]\circ H^{\mathrm{Inv}}
, so
(H.5)
implies (
4.1.41
) with
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}
. Thus we get the result
(B.1)
: a matrix-valued function
\mathfrak{B}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta^{\sim\alpha}}(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{H}^{r+2m},\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
.

Applying Lemma
4.1.21
on
H\circ L(u_{0},v_{0})=H(0,0)\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
, we can find a neighborhood
U_{1}\times V_{1}\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
of
H(0,0)
and a
G^{\prime\prime}:U_{1}\times V_{1}\to\mathbb{C}^{m}
such that

(G.1)

G^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\beta^{\sim\alpha}}(U_{1},V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
G^{\prime\prime}(H(0,0))=0
. In particular
G^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}_{(\sigma,\zeta),s}
and
\nabla_{\sigma,\zeta}G^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta^{\sim\alpha}}_{(\sigma,\zeta),s}
.

(G.2)

For each
s\in V_{1}
